# Blentera V10 verification with Claude Code

Started 2026-11-12. Continues the Codex work on draft PR #42 (`codex/blentera-v10`).
This is the status ledger for the Claude Code verification pass. Earlier history stays in
`2026-10-22-blentera-v10-recovery.md` and the dated evidence READMEs.

## Goal and decisions

- Target: the brief's **full V10 beta entry gate** (§47, brief lines 7507-7548), not a limited pilot.
- Work lands as small PRs into `codex/blentera-v10`. PR #42 stays the integration PR into `master`.
- New verification results go here or into short dated summaries, with bulky output kept in CI
  artifacts. No new folders under `doc/operations/aw-v10-evidence/`.
- Models: Opus for planning, root causes and audits; Sonnet for fixes with a clear repro;
  Haiku or Sonnet for search and logs; Fable only when Opus has failed twice (see `CLAUDE.md`).

## Starting point (head `ad190a71`, checkpoint R94)

- Requirement register: all 169 rows open.

  | Status | Rows |
  |---|---|
  | `partial_implementation_open` | 81 |
  | `no_scoped_evidence_located_open` | 39 |
  | `current_bounded_work_open` | 22 |
  | `historical_partial_evidence_open` | 17 |
  | `implemented_unqualified_open` | 10 |

- CI on the head is red. Run 43126861208:
  - about 20 of 31 managed-Native shards fail;
  - chat 1/3 and 2/3 fail, and workspaces-a 2/2 fails;
  - e2e fails.
- Run 43126861295:
  - routes-1 fails;
  - server-1 to server-4 are cancelled at the 60-minute limit;
  - changed-source security regressions and coverage fail.
- Hosting gaps:
  - public signup is closed (`deploy/v6/saas.env.example`);
  - there is no email provider;
  - billing is Paddle;
  - backups are off by default;
  - there is no Sentry DSN and no OTel configuration;
  - live UpCloud evidence is still required (`infra/README.md`).

## Phase 0 record

- `scripts/build-reference-index.mjs` writes heading indexes for the 39 playbooks
  (`doc/reference/aw-v9/playbooks/index/`) and the V10 brief (`doc/reference/blentera-v10/index.md`).
  `--check` fails when an index is stale. Re-running it produced no diff.
  `scripts/__tests__/build-reference-index.test.mjs` (5 tests) runs in `pr-trusted.yml` and fails CI when an index is stale.
- Router skills in `.claude/skills/`: `pb-security-privacy`, `pb-data-api-backend`, `pb-agentic-ai`,
  `pb-verification-reliability`, `pb-ui-ux-writing`. `doc-maintenance` is linked from `.agents/skills`.
  `check-pr` and `deal-with-security-advisory` are not linked, because they assume the `gh` CLI and
  upstream Paperclip review tooling.
- `.claude/launch.json`: removed the `cloud-web` entry. It pointed to a path outside this
  repository on another machine.
- Local checks in the cloud container, using Node 24:

  | Check | Result |
  |---|---|
  | `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` | Exit 0. It warned about missing `dist` bins for unbuilt packages. |
  | `pnpm check:token-gates` | All gates clean |
  | `pnpm check:customer-brand` | 0 leaks |
  | `server/src/__tests__/access-service.test.ts` as user `ubuntu` | 30/30 passed |

  Run as root, the same server test file reported 30 skipped. Embedded Postgres does not start
  as root, so a skipped-only run does not count as verification. `CLAUDE.md` records the
  working setup.

Foundation Context omitted under load: fixed (2026-11-18) | Root cause, not a flake: `foundationProvider` (`context-engine.ts`) ran with the generic 900 ms provider slice (`DEFAULT_CONTEXT_PROVIDER_TIMEOUT_MS`) although it now revalidates each learned revision's retained Memory roots and analytical pins (`assertLearnedAssetAnalyticalSources`), the work the Memory provider does under a deadline-derived slice. | F10c. Denial step races the Task provider's fixed 900 ms slice (found 2026-11-18) | Same case, preparation stage, after the signal source is hidden: the expected 403 `analytical_source_access_lost` comes only from Foundation (about 450 ms idle). The mandatory Task provider's `assertAnalyticalContextPayloadAccess` takes 1.1-1.2 s in that state (130-190 ms otherwise; no lock wait: `pg_blocking_pids` empty at 300 ms), so it always hits its 900 ms slice and returns 503 `Mandatory Context provider failed: task`. Which error wins is a timing race the earlier `[]` failure hid. Under CPU pressure (1 or 2 busy loops on 2 pinned cores) 3 of 6 runs failed this way; 0 of 4 on 2 pinned cores without busy loops | Server | Fixed (`55e50749`, 2026-11-19). Root cause: PostgreSQL planning, not locks or N+1. The Task reader predicate is about 225 KB of SQL (2,666-line plan); `EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS)` gives 89-93 ms planning (1,875 buffer hits) and 10-19 ms execution. The check runs six reader probes (3 from the nested native reader re-admission in `analytical-reader.ts`, 3 Memory-source batches) in three statement texts (`id = $1 ... limit`, `id in ($2)`, `id in ($2, $3)`), and with `plan_cache_mode=auto` each text is custom-planned for its first five uses on every connection: 5 binds at 94-157 ms each in the slow runs. Fix: single and batched probes share one text (`id = any($2::uuid[])`, validated UUIDs) in `authorization.ts`, and the Task provider's own transaction sets `force_generic_plan` (as the Memory privacy reader already does), so the predicate is planned once per connection and later binds plan in 0.6 ms. Only plan choice changes; every probe still reads current rows, nothing is cached, the 900 ms slice is unchanged. Provider check in the hidden state, 20 samples each: 2 pinned cores p50 350 / p90 1031 / max 1202 ms (1 of 20 assemblies 503) before, p50 372 / p90 450 / max 604 ms (0 of 20) after; 2 pinned cores + 2 busy loops p50 1217 / p90 2596 ms (7 of 8 503) before, p50 672 / p90 818 / max 958 ms (0 of 20) after. Both `:917` cases: 3/3 idle, 4/4 on 2 pinned cores, 4/4 with 1 busy loop, 13/14 runs with 2 busy loops (one 503 in the first batch of 6, then 8/8); file 42/42. `context-engine` 18/18, `context-engine-integration` 13/13, `context-manifest` 10/10, `analytical-context-privacy` 64/64, `task-privacy-prepared-plan` 2/2 (new: single/batch parity and one shared plan; removal check fails with 2 plans), `authorization-service` 88/88, `foundation-read-source-authority` 25/25, `typed-task-private-sources` 19/19, `v10-private-payload` 32/32, `context-derived-source-tombstones` 8/8, `v9-native-task-source-erasure` 12/12, `issue-native-memory-source-privacy` 1/1. `native-memory-task-transaction-reuse` not run here: its managed fixture needs `PAPERCLIP_RUNNER_REMOTE_PROVIDER_PACK` (provider pack image missing). New deny regression in `v7-learning`: the Task check alone returns 403 `analytical_source_access_lost` under the provider's planner setting. Remaining steady state is about 390 small statements (repeated identity and V5 decisions); cutting it means memoising re-admission within one check, which changes what is proven: not done |
| GW1. Rate-limit self-rejection | The dispatch re-check in `executeRemoteHttpTool` peeks the counter with `count >= limit` and counts the call's own token (`tool-access-policy.ts:1083`), so with limit N the Nth call rejects itself | `tool-gateway.test.ts`, `tool-gateway-connector-telemetry.test.ts` | Fixed in production (this PR): a server-internal token ledger, keyed by policy, bucket and window, lets only the consuming call's re-checks subtract its own token. Pre-checks and approved paths stay strict, and window rollover gives no credit. Local: 84/84 and 8/8, plus a new regression test |
| GW2–GW4. Rate-limit follow-ups | GW2: approved executions take no token at execution time, so depending on policy order they reject themselves or are not counted. GW3: the board-only `POST /companies/:id/tools/policy/test` accepts `consumeRateLimit`. GW4: the re-check returns 403 `rate_limited` while admission returns 429 | — | Fixed (2026-11-17) |
| GW2–GW4 fix (2026-11-17, spend audit) | GW2: a consuming `decide` that ends in `require_approval` releases its tokens; approved executions (execute-on-approve, workflows, agent retry, Test tab approvals) take exactly one token at execution through a ledger created with `{ approvedExecution: true }`, whatever the policy order; the approval pre-check no longer counts the request's own token; a full window at execution marks the request failed with 429 `rate_limited`; the agent retry path now runs the full policy check (stricter). GW3: `consumeRateLimit` removed from `toolPolicyTestRequestSchema` and forced false in the route (documented dry run; UI never sent it); the UI client now sends the required `companyId`. GW4: `policyRefusalError` gives re-checks and approved refusals the admission 429 shape. 19 new tests (both policy orders, window boundary, two concurrent approved executions at N-1 admit exactly one); 17 of 19 fix clauses caught by a removal check. | Server | Fixed |
| GW5. Approval-request creation no longer rate-capped (2026-11-17) | Side effect of GW2: a request waiting for approval no longer holds a token, so with the rate limit ordered first, the rate of creating approval requests is no longer capped (full-window refusal and duplicate merging remain). Decide whether approval requests need their own rate limit. | Server | Open (follow-up) |
| GW6–GW7 fix (2026-11-17, spend audit) | GW6: email publication stored no token record, so the worker's dispatch re-check counted the send's own token and refused the Nth email with 403. The admission's record is now stored with the send (`email_sends.actor.rateLimitCharge`) and dispatch re-checks with it; refusals use the shared 429; an admitted but unqueued send returns its token. GW7: exact replays of an invocation (the first-party executed-action match and idempotency-key replays) took a fresh token and refused with 403; they now re-check with the original invocation's record (`tool_invocations.policy_explanation`) and return any token just taken. Records are bound to their owner row and company, credit at most one token per policy, bucket and window, give no credit after rollover, and cannot admit a call. Plan verification passes while an execution-policy stage is pending (authority defect, high; found in the MS1f design review) | `verification-service.ts` built the Human verification packet without reading the Task's execution state, so a Human could pass plan verification (plan `completed`, Task `done`) while the Task's declared review or approval stage was still `pending`; the stage Reviewer's decision was bypassed. **Fixed (`282f8259`, MS1f decision 3, phase 1):** the packet adds the deterministic failure `execution_stage_incomplete:<stageId>` while the subject Task's stage is `pending`, so a `pass` review is recorded as `fail` (plan paused, as for every deterministic failure) and the Task stays open. **Extended (lead decision 2026-11-18, `d7e322be`):** verification passes only when every stage the Task's policy declares is completed and its latest decision is `approved`; otherwise `execution_stage_changes_requested:<stageId>` (latest decision asked for changes and the stage is not back in review) or `execution_stage_incomplete:<stageId>` (pending, not started, or completed without an approval, which includes a self-only stage the runtime auto-skips: such a policy needs a real reviewer, or the stage removed, before the plan can be verified). A pending or returned stage the policy no longer declares also blocks, and a stored policy that no longer parses fails closed (`execution_policy_invalid`). Ids only. For each completed stage it cites the latest decision when it is `approved` as `stage_decision:<decisionId>` evidence: decision id, its `updatedAt`, and a hash of {Task, stage, stage type, outcome, actor Agent/Human, creating Run, SHA-256 of the body}; never the body (the read-only model verifier already passes non-document evidence as a hash-only ref). Shared `VerificationEvidenceRef.type` gains `stage_decision`; no migration. The V7 contract is `server_arbiter`, no evidence kind existed for a Task document (`output_*` criteria), the Reviewer's later decision cannot be evidenced by the executor, and the stage starts only from an arbiter `done`. **Implemented (`f16d0475`):** (1) `supervision/task-document-output.ts`: `readTaskDocumentOutputs` and `checkTaskDocumentOutput`, extracted from the verification output check (same query, share locks, latest-revision body and number check, bounded schema check, `task_document` evidence); `verification-service.ts` uses it with no behaviour change. (2) `evidence-classifier.ts`: Task document evidence `document:<docId>:<revisionId>` (the ref verification emits), accepted only if the document is linked to this Task under a declared `task_document` required-output key, the cited revision is its latest and matches its latest body, the declared schema validates, `document_revisions.created_by_run_id` is this Run, and this Run has a succeeded `write_document` invocation with an `orchestration_tool_charges` row whose arguments hash equals the hash of the Run's server-written semantic receipt for that key and revision (the binding the gateway already uses for other Native output receipts). "Accepted" means this Run produced the current declared output, not that it is good: the evidence counts only for its own `output_<key>` criterion (`document_evidence_other_criterion`) and never as verification evidence (`document_evidence_not_verification`). Refusals: `document_missing` (not linked to this Task), `document_output_not_declared`, `document_output_missing` (empty), `document_revision_not_latest`, `document_revision_not_current`, `document_schema_mismatch`, `document_revision_not_from_run`, `document_write_not_charged`. (3) `status-arbiter.ts`: `nativeExecutionStageHandoffClaimed` and a branch after the attention and blocker branches (before the yielded and incomplete branches, so a run that is itself the incomplete continuation can hand off): disposition `done`, current contract revision, objective claimed, every criterion `satisfied` or `unknown` and none rejected (also rejects a satisfied claim with rejected evidence, and a missing claim), at least one `output_*` criterion and each accepted from its own document, no failed or rejected verification, no attention request, no blocking work, and the finalizer fact `executionStageHandoffAvailable` → `in_review`, `execution_policy_stage_handoff`, `release_checkout`; never with `agent_claim_policy`. (4) `native-stage-handoff.ts`: the executor's `done` transition, now shared by the committer's done path and the finalizer dry run (V7 `server_arbiter` contract, `activePlanCoveringTask`, transition entering a pending stage). (5) `status-decision-committer.ts` (~1760): a handoff runs the done transition under a covering plan on a non-conversation Task (same stale checks as done, now one helper) and records the existing `execution_policy_stage_pending` decision, so stage wakes, the stage source and the 0571 checks are unchanged; otherwise it commits the arbiter's fallback, which the finalizer passes (`completion_evidence_incomplete`, or `prior_status_preserved_no_live_path` after a used continuation) and the committer validates (never `done`). (6) `native-run-finalizer.ts`: computes the fact, passes the fallback, and takes the finalization phase from the applied decision for a handoff. Plan-less, `agent_claim_policy` and the reconciler's retired-review path never produce a handoff. No migration, no change to authority, spend or privacy beyond the design (the evidence reads only this Task's documents and this Run's own receipts and charges). The stage `call_api` profile is `{type: object, oneOf: [...]}` (`native-stage-comment-api.ts`); the managed OpenCode request completes a top-level `oneOf` object schema with `properties: {}` and `additionalProperties: false`, and `managedOpenCodeProviderToolsMatchCatalog` compares exactly. The catalog cannot carry those keys: the runner validates tool input against it (ajv), and a top-level `additionalProperties: false` with empty `properties` refuses every call. (b) With a temporary comparison normalization (reverted), the first Reviewer model call succeeds (stage-bound `review_model` reservation on the root plan) and its declared `read_document` executes and is charged; the next request is refused ("document source capture is incomplete"): the gateway recomputes the read's `returnedResultDigest` (SHA-256 of the DTO JSON in the owner's key order) from the provider-visible result, which the runner sends as sorted-key JSON, so it can never match (`worker-model-gateway.ts` ~2205; affects every managed OpenCode `read_document`). **Options:** (a1) restructure the stage `call_api` schema without a top-level `oneOf` (one object schema whose properties cover both shapes; the server's zod parser keeps the refinement), so the provider completion is identity; (a2) apply that two-key completion in the comparison (tried and reverted: it relaxes an exact comparison); (b1) record a canonical digest (`nativeSha256(result)`) in the read capture and compare it in the gateway (exact, additive); (b2) bind the read through the invocation's canonical result hash and consumed source entry instead (a relaxation; an automated safety check refused it); (b3) make the runner send the owner's JSON bytes. Recommendation: a1 and b1 (keep exact equality). Check for hangs before raising limits. Partly explained for `heartbeat-managed-scheduler` (2026-11-15, local, branch `conf15-work`): it is not a hang. Four cases issue a yielded `paperclip_finish` with no `response_wake` continuation. The runner input schema refuses it (`protocol/schemas/result.schema.json:154-155`), the gateway refuses the error result (`worker-model-gateway.ts:718`), the Run fails, and each case polls until its 600 s deadline. Before, the three cases at `:576`/`:705`: 600.2 s, 600.3 s and 600.1 s, all failing (1800.7 s, the 30-minute budget on its own). After adding the continuation (test-only): "plan document progress suppresses a liveness continuation" passes in 164.5 s. The two "plan-only output queues one bounded continuation" cases (600.3 s and 600.2 s) still fail: their source Run succeeds, but they wait for `livenessState: plan_only` and a `run_liveness_continuation` wake, which a yielded result with a `response_wake` continuation does not produce. Their premise (a yielded result with no continuation queues a liveness continuation) contradicts the runner schema, so their expected behaviour needs a decision. The same-Task case is CONF15-W1/W2 Plan-only liveness investigated 2026-11-15 (branch `conf15b-work`, temporary test edits and diagnostics, all removed): **no schema-valid managed OpenCode result reaches `plan_only` today, a product gap; the two cases were left unchanged (no assertion rewritten).** The closest schema-valid form of the old input is `done` with an honestly incomplete claim (criteria `unknown`, blocking remaining work, no evidence, no continuation). It reproduces the rest of the premise: the status arbiter takes its final branch (`completion_evidence_incomplete`, `status-arbiter.ts` ~498) and the `native-completion-incomplete` same-agent wakes are coalesced into the source Run, as the case expects. But `classifyRunLiveness` returns `advanced` before the plan-only branch (`run-liveness.ts:370-372`): "Run produced concrete action evidence: 1 workspace operation(s), 4 activity event(s)". The counted activity rows are written by the platform for every managed Native Run, not by the agent: `context.assembled` (bootstrap context), `issue.updated` (the arbiter's status change) and `issue.comment_added` (the run presentation comment); `environment.lease_*` and `tool_gateway.discovery` are already bookkeeping (`run-liveness.ts:6-33`). A `yielded` result must carry a continuation, and any continuation takes the arbiter's `live_continuation_registered` branch instead. Decision needed: either classify this platform activity as bookkeeping for liveness (an evidence-model change in `RUN_LIVENESS_BOOKKEEPING_ACTIVITY_ACTIONS` or `hasConcreteActionEvidence`), or retire the two cases' liveness-continuation premise for managed OpenCode Decision 2026-11-15 (coordinator): fix the gap. Implemented (branch `conf15b-work`): `isRunLivenessPlatformActivity` (`run-liveness.ts`) classifies by actor and source: `context.assembled` on a `context_manifest`, `issue.updated` by actor `system`/`native-status-committer` with source `native_status_decision`, and `issue.comment_added` with source `run_presentation_resolver`. `readRunLivenessActivityStats` (`run-liveness-evidence.ts`) excludes them in both the live classifier (`heartbeat.ts`) and the activity backfill (`activity.ts`); the Run's own tool-written comments and updates still count. Unit tests (`run-liveness.test.ts`, 24/24): platform rows plus the incomplete `done` report give `plan_only`; an agent-authored comment or update, a document write or a tool effect give `advanced`; a platform source under another actor, action or entity is not platform activity. The two scheduler cases now send the incomplete `done` report and keep their assertions. **They still fail (600 s each) at the classification poll (`:724`)**: a later Run in the same case fails before any provider call with `worker_software_host_bootstrap_closure_missing` (likely the follow-up continuation's source closure), and the poll never sees `plan_only` with exactly one `run_liveness_continuation` wake. Not diagnosed further for lack of context; next step: diagnose that Run's closure and the source Run's `livenessState` Diagnosed and fixed 2026-11-16 (branch `planonly-work`, from PR #68 head `ea757671`). With `PAPERCLIP_NATIVE_SOURCE_CLOSURE_DIAGNOSTICS=1` the failing Run is the queued `run_liveness_continuation` Run: `native_bootstrap_source_closure_unavailable:wake;builder:unsupported_or_missing_witness;wake:wake_fields_invalid:continuation+livenessContinuation`. Two gaps. (1) Test: the poll matched `payload.retryOfRunId`, but the bounded liveness record names its source as `payload.sourceRunId` (`recovery/run-liveness-continuations.ts`); `retryOfRunId` is the failed-run retry chain. Both uses (`:519`, `:718`) now read `sourceRunId`, so the `:519` negative assertion is no longer vacuous. (2) Production: the Task-wake closure had no liveness kind. `captureNativeTaskWakeSource` required `livenessContinuation: null` and `exactNativeTaskContinuation` admitted only Human-requested wakes with origin comments (`native-execution-input.ts`). Added `native-runtime/native-liveness-continuation-source.ts`: `captureNativeRunLivenessContinuationTrigger` reads the existing bounded record only (no new record or migration): receiver Run (same company; Task via context and `nativeIssueId`, or the still-unresolved legacy row during preparation), its wake (heartbeat system wake, idempotency key and exact payload, bound to the Run), the source Run (same Agent, Task, responsible Human, earlier, `succeeded`, same `livenessState`/reason/next action, `continuationAttempt` equal to the attempt) and the session (`sessionIdBefore` = source `sessionIdAfter`). `exactNativeTaskContinuation` admits the kind only with that record: heartbeat wake, no origins, the source Run's accepted finalized result as completed work through its retained-run source, and the source Run's own presentation comment only with that Run's accepted presentation owner (`execution-continuation.ts` selects it as for a withdrawal producer). `captureNativeTaskWakeSource` requires the exact `livenessContinuation` projection and binds the record into the source hash; the recurring reader (`native-direct-input.ts`) re-reads the same record. Tests: `native-liveness-continuation-source.integration.test.ts` 20/20 and a new `native-execution-input.test.ts` case; each of 52 binding checks, when removed, makes its test fail (52/52). Regression (10 files: native-execution-input, run-liveness, run-continuations, the new file, native-task-wake-continuation-source, execution-continuation, explicit-native-continuation, execution-continuation-provenance, native-execution-failure-log, native-workspace-branch-wake-source) 327/327; `tsc` server clean. Result: the classification poll now passes (`plan_only`, exactly one wake); the continuation Run passes its closure and dispatches, then its first model request is refused 403 at `worker-model-gateway.ts:210-211` (reached from the CONF15-W6 retained-history replay, `:823`): the replayed source Run's `paperclip_finish` result is an error acknowledgement (`is_error`, the incomplete `done` claim), which the retained prefix never admits. Both cases (false: 600.1 s, true: 600.1 s, each alone) now stop there, at the continuation poll (`:743`) instead of the classification poll. Full file 2381.9 s locally (2 passed, 4 failed: the two plan-only cases; the same-Task case; the dependency-comment case, fixture broker 500 at `managed-native-conversation-sandbox-host.ts:753`, an `issue_commented` path this change does not touch), so it still exceeds the 30-minute CI limit until the next stop is settled. Needs a decision: whether a retained prefix may carry the source Run's error finish acknowledgement W7 rerun 2026-11-16 (branch `p3w7-work`, with CONF15-W7): the continuation Run's first request now passes the retained-prefix check (the source Run's `paperclip_finish` is OpenCode's "Tool execution aborted" placeholder, admitted by CONF15-W7) and reaches the provider. The `false` case (alone, 600.1 s) still fails at the continuation poll (`:743`). **Next stop:** a later model request of the continuation Run (after one provider call) is refused 403 "Current Native human, internal draft Task, worker attempt and manifest required" (`worker-model-gateway.ts:2390`, `current()` via the request path `:3509`); with `PAPERCLIP_NATIVE_TEST_TOOL_RESULT_DIAGNOSTICS=1` the only failing facts are `planRunning` and `planVersionMatches` (plan `paused`, version 3, capability expects 2). Needs a decision: whether the source Run's plan-only outcome should pause the plan before its bounded continuation runs, or the continuation's capability should bind the paused plan CONF15-W8 2026-11-16 (branch `conf15n-work`, from PR #70 head `7a648980`): the plan was paused by the native tool boundary's unknown-effect fence on the continuation Run's first `write_document`, which the model-output provenance check refused because its family loop had no `retained_run` entry; fixed narrowly (row CONF15-W8). **Both plan-only cases now pass** (`false` 385.5 s, `true` 382.3 s, each alone). The same-Task case still stops at the CONF15-W7 next stop; investigated as CONF15-W9 (needs a decision). The dependency-comment case was not rerun. CONF15-W9 2026-11-16 (branch `conf15w9-work`): the same-Task case's Run 2 now passes its bootstrap closure and the canonical replacement, and its first provider call carries the current source-qualified v2 frame (past `same_task_new_run_current_v2_frame_missing`; 427.7 s alone, still failing). **Next stop:** Run 2's first `write_document` is refused by the recurring Task-wake reader; see row CONF15-W9. CONF15-W10 2026-11-16 (branch `conf15w10-work`): the same-Task case's Run 2 now gets past the recurring Task-wake reader (both wakes reconstruct; 388 s alone on CONF15-W9, 371 s with CONF15-W9R and its follow-up, still failing). **Next stop:** the Native source graph's total-work bound (2 048) in the read after Run 2's first write; see row CONF15-W10. | D5. Run identity deadlock | `captureRunIdentity` (`run-identity.ts`), run by the auth middleware on every agent-JWT request, locked the Task row and then the run row. Native source transactions (`recordNativeModelInputDispatchIntent`, `recordManagedOpenCodeToolResult` and others) take the company analytical and Memory privacy locks, then the run row, then read the Task FOR SHARE. Overlapping requests deadlocked (`40P01`); the agent's `call_api` got a 500, redacted as "Private experience request failed". Seen on #59 as the managed-browser repository-URL case; reproduced on the base too (timing-dependent). The first fix (`0189fb3b`) took both company locks in shared mode before the Task, so every agent request waited behind any exclusive Native source transaction in the company: on #59 the managed-browser file went from about 823 s to 1091 s and the repository-URL case hit its 240 s limit | Managed Native (browser) | Fixed (this PR, replaces `0189fb3b`): the per-request capture locks only the run row and no Task or company lock, so it cannot close a cycle and waits only for its own run, which still serializes it with steering delivery and acceptance. Accepting a stored but unaccepted steering acknowledgement (rare: the steering request lost its transaction) rolls back and re-locks company A+M shared > Task > run. Lock orders: capture (every request) run only; capture acceptance company shared > Task > run; identity initialize/reserve/reconcile, steering delivery, queue edits, dispatch, finalizer Task > run; Native source transactions company exclusive > lease > run > Task FOR SHARE; tool-authority mutation company exclusive > plan > Task > run; watchdog fold, warm restart, claim release run > Task (table in `run-identity.ts`). Tests: the regression test still deadlocks on the pre-`0189fb3b` code; new tests show capture completes while another run's source transaction holds both company locks (fails on `0189fb3b`), waits for steering delivery and returns the accepted identity, and releases the run before acceptance (top-level and nested). Still open: Task > run transactions without company locks (identity reserve/reconcile, steering delivery) can still deadlock with Native source transactions. The managed case cannot finish locally (provider-pack staging takes about 120 s and the environment lease expires, the same on `0189fb3b`), so the CI duration is the check |

It refuses a symlink, a special file or a multiply linked file anywhere, a symlinked ancestor on the source or target side (`path_escape`), more than 20,000 entries or 256 MiB (`bound_exceeded`), and a different copy already in Run 2's folder (`target_conflict`); files are read with O_NOFOLLOW|O_NONBLOCK against the inspected inode. The copy's digest (path, type, mode, size, SHA-256 of every entry) must equal the source's, and the source must be unchanged after the copy (`digest_mismatch`). The executor fails closed with `native_session_carry_<code>`; an unexpected I/O error is `copy_failed`. Run 2 keeps its own per-Run folder; nothing is shared. **Cascade.** `eraseNativeRuntimeAssets` (`:1199`; Run 1's existing outbox job, so Source erasure, the Memory worker and company purge all reach it) now lists every later Run of the same company, Agent, Task and `native_session_id` (`managedOpenCodeCarryDescendants`, `:799`; no record proves provenance, so all of them) and applies the existing Stop gate to each before removing anything (`assertManagedOpenCodeCarryDescendantStopped`, `:813`): a non-terminal Run, a live process, process group or controller lease fails the job, which is retried exactly as an unstopped owner is; a later Run with a provider home also needs a settled finalization and the local Stop receipt or the canonical remote Stop. It then removes Run 1's run root and each later Run's `provider-opencode` only (Run 2's own runtime context stays). No new lock in the erasure path, no migration, no new record. Company purge and offboarding are unchanged (`enqueueCompanyManagedOpenCodeRuntimeErasure` still gives every Run its own job, and `assertCompanyManagedOpenCodeRuntimeDataErased` still requires every provider home gone). The EN/DA `export-and-delete` help article now says a later Run's copy is erased once that Run has stopped. **Tests.** `native-session-carry.integration.test.ts` 29/29 (embedded Postgres, real files): carry positive (exact copy, modes, nothing outside `workspace`/`data`, Run 1 unchanged, idempotent `already_present`) and 22 refusals (no handoff; handoff for another Run or session; prior Run of another company, Agent, Task or session; the current Run itself; a later Run; a non-terminal or non-OpenCode prior; Run 1 erasure queued or succeeded; an earlier Run's erasure; Run 1's folder gone; incomplete data; tampered copy; source changed during the copy; symlink; FIFO; hard link; source and target path escape; bound; target conflict); cascade (Run 1's root and Run 2's carried home removed; earlier Run, other Agent, session, Task and company untouched; idempotent re-run; an active later Run defers the direct call and the worker job, a terminal later Run without its Stop receipt defers, both succeed after its Stop; company purge removes all). Check removal: each of 33 checks removed one at a time (24 carry, 9 cascade) makes its test fail. Regression: `runtime-asset-retention.test.ts`, `memory-jobs.test.ts`, `native-cold-lease-handoff`, `native-session-executor.test.ts`, `native-harness-backup-cleanup.test.ts`, `legacy-runtime-assets`, `company-offboarding-revoke-lease`, `v6-company-purge`, `v8-company-purge` with the new file 574/574; `analytical-context-privacy` 63/63 (Memory Source erasure of a managed provider home). Not evidence either way: `native-payload-temporal-sources` (managed; erases two Runs' provider homes) fails locally in setup before its erasure case both with this change (Sandbox binding "Authenticate again to continue") and without it on `1bc10897` (`managed_fixture_context_prepare_failed`, context deadline). `tsc` server clean. **Acceptance (both lanes pass the CONF15-R stop).** Worker same-Task case: Run 2 carries, recovers the OpenCode session and sends its first model request; the dispatch recorder then refuses it with `managed_opencode_source_prompt_closure_unavailable` (`native-session-executor.ts:8303-8306`), reason `wake_binding` (`nativeTurnFrameMatchesSourceClosure`, `:8055`): Run 2's bootstrap source closure holds two `native_task_wake` inputs, Run 1's (other `runId`) and its own, and the check allows at most one, bound to this Run. Six-probe case: probe 2 (`resume`) carries and `recoverSession` now succeeds; `attachRun` (`native-opencode-conformance.ts:333`, transport `runnerd-codex-transport.ts:3902`) asks the executor's control-plane registration (`native-session-executor.ts:17898`) to register this Run's PRP route again, and `registerRunnerPrpAuthority` refuses `runner_prp_authority_already_registered` (`server/src/realtime/runner-prp-ws.ts:130-131`), because the recovered runner already registered that route for the same Run id. Both next stops need a decision (a retained Run's wake as a second wake source; re-registering a Run's own PRP route on attach). The remote-placement observation moved to PRIV-R1. Previous status: Open; needs a decision. Options: (A) carry Run 1's exact session root from Run 1's retained folder into Run 2's per-Run folder after a verified cold handoff (digest-checked, no symlinks or special files), and extend the managed erasure so that erasing a Run also erases (after their Stop) the provider homes of later Runs of the same `heartbeat_runs.native_session_id` that carried from it. Existing columns, no migration, but an erasure-semantics change in `eraseNativeRuntimeAssets` and the `memory-jobs.ts` worker. (B) make the managed OpenCode data home session-scoped with a session erasure owner: a retention-model change. (C) do not resume the provider session across Runs for managed OpenCode: open a fresh session and rely on the source-qualified frame, as managed conversations do. That changes the Worker test's same-`providerSessionId` expectation and what the conformance `resume` probe proves. Recommendation: (A), because it keeps per-Run folders and Run-scoped Stop gates and only adds the descendant cascade. Separate observation: for a real remote sandbox, the managed runtime folder is a controller path handed to the remote process unmapped, and the remote erasure removes only `sessions/<digest>`. Remote placement and erasure of managed OpenCode data are unproven by the software fixture |

| CONF15-P2. Conformance six-probe: attach after recover re-registers the Run's own PRP route (found after CONF15-R, 2026-11-16) | Probe 2 (`resume`): after `recoverSession`, `attachRun` (`native-opencode-conformance.ts:333`, transport `runnerd-codex-transport.ts:3902`) calls the executor's control-plane registration (`native-session-executor.ts:17898` before CONF15-W3, now `:17954-17958`) for the same Run id, and `registerRunnerPrpAuthority` refuses `runner_prp_authority_already_registered` (`realtime/runner-prp-ws.ts:130-131`). Cause: recovery starts runnerd under this Run's own PRP identity (`prpIdentity.runId = execution.binding.runId`, `native-session-executor.ts:17773-17780`), so the attach is a same-Run rotation (diagnostic: `prior.runId === desired.runId`). **Tried (lead's rule), then reverted.** An identical-registration reuse in the registry (same authority object, binding digest over company, Task, Agent, Run, bound Run, runner instance, lease and session; live authority identity; current live generation; authenticated connection; ref-counted release handles) with 16 new tests in `runner-prp-ws.test.ts` (31/31), each of its 12 checks shown necessary by removal. With it the registration passes, but `run.attach` then stays pending with zero runner connections until `provider_transport_failed: PRP command run.attach timed out` (`runnerd-codex-transport.ts:5994`): runnerd itself refuses a same-Run attach, "run.attach authority changed an immutable session binding" when `next.run_id == current.run_id` (`packages/paperclip-runner/runner/crates/runner-core/src/durable/runner.rs:199-206`). So making this safe needs more than registration reuse, and by the decision rule the change was reverted (no code from this attempt is committed). **Next stop (unchanged):** `realtime/runner-prp-ws.ts:130-131`, and behind it `runner.rs:199-206`. Options: (a) the transport's `attachRun` treats a same-Run attach after recover as a no-op (no registration, no `run.attach`) when the core's identity already names this Run, runner instance, lease and session, and only the session layer adopts the new turn and item ids; (b) the conformance driver (or `HarnessDriverBackend.attachRun`) skips `attachRun` when the recovered session already carries this Run's identity; (c) recover under the previous Run's PRP identity and let `attachRun` perform the real cross-Run rotation runnerd supports (changes which authority the recovered runner first binds to). Recommendation: (a) or (b), smallest; (c) changes recovery authority **2026-11-16 (lead decision: option (b)) stopped, not implemented.** The recover result does not carry server-verifiable identity for the current Run: `HarnessDriverBackend.recoverSession` returns `{ recovered: true, session }` with the session built from the snapshot the server sent (`new HarnessNativeSession({ identity: snapshot.identity }, ...)`, `packages/paperclip-runner/src/backends/harness-driver-backend.ts:182-183`), and that snapshot is probe 1's (`native-opencode-conformance.ts:993`), so its `runId` is the previous Run's; neither the result nor the session carries the runner instance, lease, or the Run runnerd was started under (that is only in the transport's private core state, set from the executor's `prpIdentity`, `native-session-executor.ts:17773-17780`; the managed wrapper returns OpenCode sessions unchanged, `:18076`). Skipping `attachRun` would also leave `session.identity().runId` at the previous Run, so the probe's identity check (`native-opencode-conformance.ts:334-336`) would refuse, and the recovered terminal would not be reset. By the decision rule no code was changed. Rerun 2026-11-16 (with CONF15-W4): probe 2 (`resume`) still fails in step `session` with `runner_prp_authority_already_registered` (`realtime/runner-prp-ws.ts:131`, via `native-session-executor.ts:17952-17956` and transport `attachRun` `runnerd-codex-transport.ts:3902`). Making (b) safe needs the recover result to carry a runner-confirmed binding (company, Run, session, runner instance, lease) that the server can compare with what it sent, which changes the backend/transport contract **2026-11-16 design comparison (c) vs (d), read-only.** Common fact: after CONF15 A2 both lanes recover a cross-Run session by the server-verified cold lease handoff, not by `run.attach`: the executor archives Run 1's control-plane and runner identity (`native-session-executor.ts:17496-17540`) and passes `coldLeaseHandoff`; the transport's recovery (`#resume`, `packages/paperclip-runner/src/live/runnerd-codex-transport.ts:4891`) then seeds a fresh runner bound to `prpIdentity` = this Run, this Run's lease (`:5046-5084`; `prpIdentity.runId = execution.binding.runId`, `native-session-executor.ts:17773-17780`; lease `coldLeaseHandoff.currentEnvironmentLeaseId`, `:15795`). No Worker code calls `attachRun`; its only callers are the two conformance drivers (`native-opencode-conformance.ts:333`, `native-provider-conformance.ts:81`). So probe 2's `attachRun` repeats a binding the cold handoff already made. **(c) recover under Run 1, then a real cross-Run attach.** Today: the PRP registry is keyed by Run id (`realtime/runner-prp-ws.ts:130`), so registering Run 2 after Run 1 does not collide, and each registration makes its Run the live command authority for the Task and Agent (`:143-153`); the transport's `attachRun` keeps the prior runner instance and lease and changes only Run, turn and item (`runnerd-codex-transport.ts:3895-3900`) and needs `#startupComplete` (`:3890`); runnerd accepts a cross-Run attach only with the same runner instance, lease and session and a new Run (`runner.rs:199-206`), and only after the old outbox and v2 session state are acknowledged (`:218-227`). Consequences: Run 2 would run on Run 1's lease, which conflicts with per-Run leases and with A2's proof that Run 1's runner stopped and its lease was released with a termination receipt (`native-session-executor.ts:15763-15775`, verifier `:15088`); A2's handoff (and CONF15-R's data carry, `:15778`) would have to be skipped for the probe; Run 1, a terminal Run, would again hold a live PRP route and command authority until the attach; and the probe lane would diverge from the Worker lane, which never attaches. Files: the executor's recovery identity and handoff (`native-session-executor.ts`), the conformance driver, and, to move the lease to Run 2's, the transport's `attachRun` plus runnerd's immutable-binding rule (Rust, `runner.rs:199-206`), i.e. a pack and runner change needing separate approval. Tests: runnerd rotation, registry handover, lease settlement, the six-probe case. **(d) runner-confirmed binding on the recover result.** Add an optional `binding` (company, Run, normalized session, runner instance, environment lease) to the recover result, taken by the transport from its durable core identity only after runnerd authenticated on this Run's route (the identity `#resume` set, `:5037-5044`, `:5169-5170`), carried by `HarnessDriverBackend.recoverSession` (`harness-driver-backend.ts:158-190`) and the OpenCode driver; the conformance driver compares it with what the server sent (probe Run, the conformance runner instance, the cold-handoff lease, the session) and, only on an exact match, adopts the probe identity on the recovered session without `run.attach` and with no new registration; any absent or different field refuses. The session needs a narrow adopt step (identity to the confirmed Run, clear the recovered terminal, as `attachRun` does at `:493-495`). Authority: no new authority; the server checks runner-reported facts against its own records, and the PRP route stays single-Run. Worker lane: no change in behaviour (additive field; it may later assert the same binding). Files: `harness-driver-backend.ts`, the driver and session types in `packages/paperclip-runner/src`, `runnerd-codex-transport.ts`, the OpenCode server driver, `native-opencode-conformance.ts:330-336` (and `native-provider-conformance.ts:81` for parity). Pack: TS only, in the provider pack's `dist` (digest changes; built from source per CI run, CONF15-P); no Rust or protocol change. Tests: transport binding present only after authentication and equal to the core identity; backend passes it through; driver refusals for missing binding and other Run, session, runner instance, lease or company; positive adopt; the six-probe case rerun. **Recommendation: (d).** It keeps A2's per-Run lease and termination proof, matches the Worker lane's cold-handoff path, needs no runner (Rust) change, and turns option (b)'s missing proof into a checked binding. It still changes the backend/transport contract and the provider pack digest, so it needs approval before implementation **2026-11-16 option (d) implemented (lead approval).** The recover result carries an optional runner-confirmed `binding` (Run, normalized session, runner instance, environment lease; `HarnessConfirmedRunBinding`, `packages/paperclip-runner/src/contracts/harness-driver.ts`). The transport's `confirmedRunBinding()` (`runnerd-codex-transport.ts:3882-3906`) reads it only after startup completed, with no failure or warm transition pending, from the core's `authenticatedRunnerIdentity()` (`control-plane/durable-prp-control-plane.ts:1608-1639`): the identity runnerd presented in `auth_hello` on the single live authenticated connection, equal to the core's current identity. It is never read from the snapshot. The Codex-compatible driver adds it to its recover result (`codex-app-server-driver-impl.ts:624-633`); `HarnessDriverBackend.recoverSession` passes it through only when well formed (exactly four non-empty strings) and never synthesizes one (`harness-driver-backend.ts:181-197`). Adopt step: `HarnessNativeSession.adoptConfirmedRun` (one use; the passed binding must equal the session's own recovered binding; identity Run and session equal to it; session, company, Task and Agent unchanged; clears the recovered terminal as `attachRun` does) and `CodexHarnessSession.adoptConfirmedRun` (idle session only; the live transport binding must name this Run and session; drops reconstruction events labelled with the snapshot's Run; same Run-scoped resets as `attachRun`, shared through `#rebindRun`; no `run.attach`). Conformance driver (`native-opencode-conformance.ts:283-302, 355-370`): compares the binding field by field with what the server sent for this probe Run (probe Run and session, the conformance runner instance, and the Run's own EnvironmentRun lease `remote.leaseId`, now passed as `environmentLeaseId`) via `nativeOpenCodeConformanceRecoveredBindingMatches`; on an exact match it adopts and skips `run.attach`; a missing or different binding, or a session without the adopt step, refuses (`managed_opencode_resume_binding_unconfirmed` / `_adopt_unavailable`). Refusal, not a fallback to attach: attach is known to collide with the Run's own PRP route, and attaching after an unexpected binding would rotate an unconfirmed runner's authority onto this Run. | CONF15-P4. Conformance six-probe: per-probe recent authentication (CONF15-P3 next stop, 2026-11-16) | Probe 3 (`peer_unseen`) failed in step `open` with `recent_authentication_required` about 307 s in: every probe's Sandbox bootstrap (`execution-sandbox/sandbox-service.ts` `assertNativeConformanceBootstrap`) and conformance host command (`native-host-bridge.ts` conformance `authorize`) required the session to be authenticated within `RECENT_AUTHENTICATION_SECONDS` (300) of that probe. **How production starts a qualification:** one Human request (`POST` on `routes/agent-provider-bindings.ts` → `providerConformanceService.test` → `nativeOpenCodeConformanceService.test`) creates the group id and both hidden harnesses and runs all six probes serially in that request; no per-probe Human action exists. So every real qualification longer than five minutes failed. **Change (brief rule: bind to the group's start).** `assertNativeConformanceHumanAuthentication` (`execution-sandbox/native-conformance-authentication.ts`) keeps the recent check first; otherwise the session must still be valid and unrevoked (`assertHumanAuthentication` without the recent bound: expiry, authenticator version, deletion, method) and `nativeConformanceGroupAuthenticationAdmits` must hold over server rows only: the same group and plan on the company-scoped Run, the Run bound to the hidden Task and the Task to the Run, the same Human on the Run, plan creator and plan principal, a running provider-conformance Run, a paused unerased plan and an in-progress hidden provider-conformance Task (not cancelled or finished), a session whose own `authenticated_at` was within 300 s before the group's plan `startedAt`, and the group's own deadline (`startedAt + maxWallClockSeconds`) not reached. Both owners above use it. **No group record holds a session reference** (the group is an in-request id; the harness plans' `executionPrincipal` JSON is strict), so "the same session" is bound by its authentication time relative to the group's start, not by session id: a second session of the same Human authenticated within the five minutes before the group started would also pass. Binding the exact session would need a column; not done. | CONF15-W11. Worker same-Task Run 2: the source graph's total-work bound in the read after Run 2's first write (CONF15-W10 next stop, 2026-11-16) | **Decision (lead): keep the 2 048-unit bound (a DoS/resource guard). Inside one read call, identical source validations may be memoized: each distinct source input plus its exact validation context is validated once, later uses reuse that proof, and work is counted for distinct validations only; the memo never crosses calls, Runs, transactions or companies. Implemented 2026-11-16 (branch `conf15w11-work` from `conf15w10-work` `25a4f5c2`, with `f82f4d1d` from PR #72 cherry-picked).** **Root cause** (temporary content-free work trace of the refused read, removed). The refused read is Run 2's managed consumed-set walk (`assertNativeManagedConsumedSourceSetCurrentInScope`) with three components: the bootstrap (18 inputs); Run 2's first dispatch, whose checked inputs (its marker removed) are exactly the bootstrap set; and the dispatch after its first write (that write's result and revision). Run 2's comment wake carries the W9 inert plan context, so its reconstruction (`assertNativeTaskWakeSourceCurrentInScope`) takes the ordinary plan read, which calls `disableNativeSourceGraphCheckpoints` (`native-direct-input.ts:3063` at `25a4f5c2`, "no reusable proof family") and turns graph proofs off for the rest of the call, here at unit 123. From then on every identical set was walked again. The bootstrap component cost 549 units. The identical dispatch component cost 886: its graph proof existed but was off. The third component revalidates Run 2's own revision through its capture, so it walks the 18-input set again and, under every wake rebuild, the retained Run's seven receipt sets; it crossed 2 048 after 611 more units. (The CONF15-W10 row described the stack at the refusal, which is inside this third component.) **Change.** `native-runtime/native-exact-call-source-proofs.ts` adds a store created with each read ancestry, which owns the work counter. Proofs are owned by the exact read scope object and keyed by the validation's source key, depth hint and exact context. The store is bounded (512 per scope), and the first effect boundary (`prepareNativeSourceEffectBoundary`) closes it for the rest of the call: nothing recorded before the boundary is read after it, and a validation that spans it is never recorded. `withNativeSourceGraphProof` consults the store when no graph proof applies, including while graph proofs are off. A hit costs no work unit. It schedules the current reader checks again and gives the parent frames its reader sets, mutable rows and descendants, exactly as a graph proof hit does. It is refused for ineligible inputs or an incompatible proof, and a proof whose descendants are active is walked again, which then finds the cycle. Only the payload source-set validator passes an exact context (`nativePayloadSourceSetExactCallContext`: the validated dispatch sources, sorted, and the consumer cover). Its source key already holds company, Task, Run, actor, reader scope, Memory prefix, policy, inputs and historical transition. The bound, the graph proofs and every other owner are unchanged. No migration, Rust, pack, spend or accounting change. **Tests.** `native-exact-call-source-proofs.test.ts` 16/16, through the real wrapper and work counter (`nativeSourceGraphProofWorkProbeForTest`). Positives: duplicates are counted once with graph proofs off (regression: 101 units for one, two or three identical validations, 303 without the change), and across callers' proof maps with graph proofs on (the same-map graph proof still charges its unit); a nested identical set inside distinct parents is reused. Refusals: 21 distinct sets of 100 units refuse at the bound, also when each one repeats, and one set over the bound refuses. Nothing is reused for another depth hint, validated dispatch sources, consumer cover, actor or source key (the same dispatch sources in another order are reused). Nothing leaks across calls, nothing is reused after or across an effect boundary, and nothing is memoized for ineligible inputs, an incompatible proof or an owner without an exact context. A reuse schedules the reader checks again, and its parent gets the reused proof's readers, mutable rows and descendants. An active descendant walks again and finds the cycle. Store: keys, owner isolation, close and bound. The set validator is the only exact-context caller. Removal: 21/24 conditions, each removed alone, fail a test (a no-change control passes). The other 3 are redundant guards: the read-side closed check and the clear on close mask each other, and the eligibility guard on the key is masked by the record gate inside `if (eligible)`. | CONF15-P7. Conformance six-probe: probe 6 (`cancel`) fails `managed_opencode_cancel_not_observed` (CONF15-P6 next stop, 2026-11-16) | **Diagnosis** (temporary content-free diagnostics, removed: the broker's static 403 message with ids masked and its frames, the probe's event types and cancel flags, and the wire wait's ledger read). The 403 is the gateway's own answer to an aborted dispatch: `callNativeOpenCodeConformanceMessages` (`orchestration/native-opencode-conformance-admission.ts:343`) returns "The conformance dispatch outcome is unknown; retain the full reservation liability". It came 30.5 s after the dispatch, when the held provider request was aborted (the fixture holds the cancel probe's response until it is aborted). It is a consequence, not the refusing gate. The cancel was never requested. Probe 6's events were `turn.submitted`, `turn.started` and `turn.accepted`, then `run.result.proposed` and `turn.failed` at 33.3 s, with `cancellationRequested: false`. The wire wait (`waitForNativeOpenCodeConformanceWire`, `native-opencode-conformance.ts` ~207) read the reservation once as `dispatched` with no wire receipt and returned false. **Root cause (wiring).** The reservation ledger persists a dispatch in two steps. `claimForDispatch` sets `dispatched` (`orchestration/model-reservations.ts:1154`); then, after two harness revalidations and the credential read, `recordWireDispatch` retains the receipt (`:1244`). The wait treated only a reserved row without a receipt as the pre-wire state, so a poll between the two steps read "not an exact wire" and gave up. This needs no new authority rule: the cancel stays gated on the exact persisted receipt. **Change** (`native-opencode-conformance.ts:209`). A reserved or claimed (`dispatched`) row without a receipt is the pre-wire state. The cancel is still scheduled only after the exact persisted POST receipt (version, reservation, request hash, method, endpoint and body digest) on a `dispatched` or `unknown` row. Every other row still returns false, and the stream end and the probe signal still stop the wait. No migration, Rust, pack, spend or accounting change. **Tests.** `native-opencode-conformance-cancel.test.ts` 6/6 (4 new). Regression: null, reserved, claimed without a receipt, then the exact receipt: the wait goes through the claimed row and cancels once; an `unknown` row after the exact receipt also cancels. Refusals: six non-exact receipts on a dispatched row; a claimed row that settles without a receipt (`unknown`, `failed`, `cancelled` or `completed`), or a receipt on a settled or reserved row, gives up promptly without a cancel; a claimed row stops at the stream end or an aborted signal. Removal: 4/4 conditions, each removed alone, fail a test (a no-change control passes); without the change the regression test fails. Regression and `tsc`: row CONF15-W11. **Reruns** (six-probe case alone, provider pack). The diagnostic run (CONF15-W11 code, before this change; 911.4 s, the file 939.6 s) passes probes 1 to 5, and probe 6 stops as above. After the change, two runs (344.3 s and 344.2 s; the file 377.0 s and 376.0 s) **stop earlier**: probe 3 (`peer_unseen`), step `open`, `sandbox_stop_unconfirmed` (`sandbox-service.ts:1551` via `:1677`: the peer Host's admission outcome is unknown, and its failed Host commands include `prepare` with `sandbox_scope_changed`). The base code stops the same way (the `25a4f5c2` versions of both changed files: 350.5 s, the file 385.1 s), so neither CONF15-W11 nor this change causes it; the diagnostic run on the same CONF15-W11 code passed probe 3 about 30 minutes earlier. Not diagnosed further (outside this rule), so probe 6 has not yet been observed in a managed run after the change. **Next stop:** probe 3's peer Host admission in this environment. Needs investigation. | Server 17 | Implemented; managed rerun stops earlier (probe 3) |
| CONF15-P7b. Conformance six-probe: probe 6 (`cancel`) receipt step is timing-dependent | On #73 (`7f318115`, run 43961938972) attempt 1 completed all six probes; attempt 2 on the same commit stopped at probe 6, `native_opencode_conformance_failed {stage: probe, probeKind: cancel, step: receipt, errorCode: null}` (test line 158). So the P7 wire wait still races the cancel against dispatch/receipt in some orderings. Root cause: the model broker, not the probe, writes the reservation outcome (`recordOutcome`: `dispatched` to `completed`/`unknown`/`failed`) in its own request handler after the runner's request aborts; the probe's receipt step read the row once, so when it read first the row was still `dispatched` with the exact receipt and the step threw 403. Fix (`native-opencode-conformance.ts`, `readNativeOpenCodeConformanceProbeReservations`): for the cancel probe only, re-read every 100 ms while the single new row is `dispatched` with a receipt, up to 60 s; the strict proof moved unchanged into `nativeOpenCodeConformanceProbeReservationProven` (a row still `dispatched` at the bound, `failed` or `cancelled` still fails). No spend, cost or authority change. `native-opencode-conformance-cancel.test.ts` 10/10 (4 new); removal check: one-read version fails 2 tests with the CI failure mode. | Managed Native 9 | Implemented; awaiting CI |
| CONF15-P8. Fix: the serial owner retains every Host it creates (retired or not) and the router adds the combined observations (`collectRetainedConformanceObservations`, conformance mode only); rows are copied, never deduplicated, so the exact `toHaveLength(6)` and tuple checks keep their strength. No production change. New unit test 2/2; serial-fixture test 3/3; typecheck clean. :167 not yet reached locally (the local six-probe run stops at probe 3, environmental). | F8 fix. create-wait test deadlocks its own 3-connection pool (2026-11-18) | `native-opencode-managed-project-create-wait.integration.test.ts` timed out at 240 s on #54, #58, #66, #71, #83, #84 and #85 (base tree too). In the CI log (#85, job 134666024711) the host progress lines stop about 75 s into the case and the run stays `running`; in another hung run the event log stops at `harness.diagnostic` (seq 8). **Cause (test coordinator, no production fault):** the test held the Task row `FOR UPDATE` and then looked for the constructor's lock wait through `db`, the same `maxConnections: 3` pool the run uses (old test lines ~105-112 and ~131). While the lock is held, all three run connections can end up blocked behind it: the Project constructor (`FOR SHARE` on the Task, `assertNativeTaskSourceInputCurrentInScope`, `native-direct-input.ts:6780`), a concurrent Task source check, or a run-row `select ... for update` queued behind the constructor. The probe then waits for a free pool slot forever and never releases the lock; Postgres sees no deadlock because the lock holder is idle in its transaction, so the run stays `running` and the progress sampler, which uses the same pool, goes quiet. The MS1h7 change (`control.ts`) is not involved. Shown with temporary diagnostics (removed): a separate client sampled `pg_stat_activity`, and an added 6 s hold before the probe gave a deterministic repro: 2/2 hung (pool: blocker idle in transaction; constructor and a second run connection in `Lock transactionid`, blocked by the blocker or by the constructor), against 4/4 passes without the hold. **Fix (test only, `6f3cb472`):** the hook reads, the lock-holding transaction, the Task edit (`issueService(controlDb).update(..., tx)`) and the wait probe use a separate 2-connection client (`controlDb`). The run, its guards and every assertion are unchanged; no timeout changed. After: 5/5 passes (139-150 s) and 2/2 with a 6 s and a 15 s hold. | Managed Native 1 | Fixed (2026-11-18, test only) |

The route commits the statuses before it answers (`issue-tree-control.ts` → `cancelIssueStatusesForHold`), but on a loaded runner that transaction took about 2.5 s and one `GET /api/issues/:id` took 1-3.5 s, so the last read that finished in time had started before the commit. Reproduced locally (1 of 8 under CPU load); a status trigger showed both Tasks go `blocked -> cancelled` once, with no later write. #96 does not touch this path; base `9ca4b4b1` passed 8 of 8 under the same load (timing margin, not a regression) | e2e | Fixed in the test: wait for the cancel response (201, mode `cancel`, both Tasks not skipped), then read each status once. Other 5 s waits in this test (`Subtree is paused.` after reload, the `blocked` poll) can still fail on a heavily loaded machine; not seen in CI |
| SS-C3. Managed sign-off case 3 executor interrupted after its terminal result on #97 (2026-11-19) | `managed-signoff-api-policy.integration.test.ts:1132` failed on #97 (job 135917948702): only 1 Run. The executor reached `run.result.accepted` and `run.terminal`, then ended `failed` with the catch-all `native_session_interrupted` (stored code "unrecognized", no turn failures, no exec failure record); the Reviewer Run never started and the test's 360 s polling deadline expired. #97 ran on an Azure westus3 runner (#96 on AWS us-west-2); sandbox-side `runner.artifact.prepare` took 116-151 s there against 55-71 s on #96 and session startup about 145 s per Run, so two Runs would not fit in 360 s either way. | EXC1. Exec claims queue behind long exclusive company-lock holders | Every sandbox exec runs `assertTaskExecutionContext` (company analytical + memory keys shared, then its row checks) in its initial and claim transactions, and relay authorization (`authorizeSandboxExec`) takes both company keys exclusively. A Native dispatch holds the keys exclusively and the run row (FOR UPDATE, then its UPDATE) through its whole source-owner recheck; about 140 other writers take the keys exclusively; an exclusive request queued behind a long shared reader makes later shared requests queue too. Sampled locally: waits on `pg_advisory_xact_lock_shared(hashtextextended('business-events:<company>'))` and on `heartbeat_runs ... for share` (transactionid lock of the dispatch's run update), both behind the dispatch's privacy recheck. On #97's slow runner the 2 s runner process identity read (`waitForRemoteRunnerProcessIdentity`) ran out after its claim was CLAIMED, which fenced the Task attempt (case 1, line 699). EXC2 removes the fencing, not the queueing | Managed Native | Open, deferred. Real fix: a per-company privacy generation counter bumped by every exclusive writer, so the dispatch can run its long recheck outside the exclusive window and prove nothing changed before it commits. Needs a migration and changes to about 140 writers |
| EXC2. Exec admission never claims past its deadline (2026-11-19) | `authorizeSandboxExec` (`runtime/control.ts`) sets `SET LOCAL lock_timeout` to the caller's remaining exec time minus `SANDBOX_EXEC_CLAIM_RELAY_FLOOR_MS` (250 ms, the least time the relay needs after the claim; an eighth of the 2 s identity read), at the start and again after the company locks; refuses (`exec_timeout`) when that time is already gone; checks it again right before both PENDING -> CLAIMED updates (general and remote-erasure paths); maps 55P03 to `exec_timeout`. A refusal rolls back with the row PENDING; `executeSandboxTask`'s catch then sets it FAILED (a known outcome, not a fence; the erasure caller sets EXPIRED). No deadline, authority check or `statement_timeout` changed (lock waits are the only unbounded part; the check before the update covers the rest). Tests in `v7-sandbox-host-bridge.integration.test.ts` (signed Sandbox Task): a company reader holding the analytical key shared for 3 s makes a 1.5 s exec end `exec_timeout` in under 3 s, row FAILED; a dispatch-like writer holding both keys exclusively leaves no PENDING/CLAIMED row; lease and run row waits after the company locks that each fit `lock_timeout` but together pass the claim budget still end FAILED (deadline aimed past a whole second so the connector broker JWT expiry cannot refuse first); the next exec runs to exit 0. Removal checks, each fails the test: no `lock_timeout` (reader waited out, wrong error), no check before the update (row left CLAIMED), no 55P03 mapping (raw lock error). The erasure-path check has no test (same helper; no erasure fixture in this test) | Server | Fixed (mitigation; EXC1 stays open). Related suites: bridge, run-identity, v7-sandbox, execution-sandbox routes, reviewer sandbox owner, conformance source authority, review credential transaction: 264/264 |
| D5c. Exec checks and Task-before-run writers could deadlock (found 2026-11-19, pre-existing) | `assertTaskExecutionContext` (exec initial, claim, relay authorization, output and result checks) locked the run FOR SHARE and then the Task FOR SHARE (`authority()`), under shared company keys. Steering acceptance in `captureRunIdentity` (company shared > Task FOR UPDATE > run FOR UPDATE) and the Task > run writers without company keys (identity initialize/reserve/reconcile, steering delivery, queue edits, dispatch, finalizer, restart recovery) could close a cycle with it. **Fixed:** after the lease, a plain read finds the run's Task id and the Task is locked FOR SHARE before the run; every check still reads the same locked rows. The lock-order table in `run-identity.ts` has the new row and the argument. **D5d (follow-up, same day):** the run > Task writers without company keys now lock the Task first too, so every listed writer orders company > Task > run: the watchdog fold (`foldSourceResolvedRun`) locks the source Task with its update's lock mode and predicate before the run update; warm restart verification (`verifyWarmTransitionRestart`) takes its existing Task FOR UPDATE read before the run and coordinator (the finalizer's Task > run > coordinator order); claim release (`releaseRunClaimedJustBeforeSuppression`, moved to module level) reads the run's Task unlocked, locks it, updates the run, and if the run now names another Task rolls back and retries once, then proceeds as before. No read, decision or write changed. Still run first (found while auditing, not moved): the finalization reconciler's resume claim (run + coordinator FOR UPDATE, then the Task in its ambiguous-state branch; reached only for a run no runner owns and with no process) and the hidden conformance backend check (shared locks only, which never conflict with the exec checks). New `run-task-lock-order.test.ts`: each moved writer races the exec checks' order behind a Task holder; no 40P01 (4 tests, including the claim-release retry, which gives the old outcome); reverting each reorder fails its test with 40P01 (3/3). Writers' suites: watchdog (adapter, use cases, policy, process, output watchdog), worktree/scheduling suppression, task drain, native session executor: 588 passed; the warm-transition integration suite (35) is skipped, as on CI: it needs `PAPERCLIP_WARM_TRANSITION_FIXTURE_DIRECTORY`, which no workflow sets. New barrier test in the bridge test: the acceptance order holds the Task, the exec check waits on it, then the acceptance takes the run: both complete; on the old order the exec check is aborted with 40P01 | Server | Fixed (D5c, D5d); open: the reconciler resume claim and the conformance backend check still take the run first |

The warm-transition restart integration suite never runs (found 2026-11-20) | `server/src/__tests__/native-warm-transition-restart.integration.test.ts` (35 tests) skips unless `PAPERCLIP_WARM_TRANSITION_FIXTURE_DIRECTORY` and `PAPERCLIP_TEST_DATABASE_URL` are set; no workflow or script sets them, so CI reports it as skipped and it has never verified anything there. A skipped suite is not a passing suite | CI | Open: wire a fixture directory and database into a CI job (no test change), then record its first real result |

## Decisions taken (2026-11-12)

The owner delegated all technical and product decisions to the verification lead (Claude).
These are the defaults. A later decision that changes one updates this table.

| Topic | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| Hosting | Keep UpCloud plus HCP Terraform (`deploy/v6`, `infra/`). Staging first, then production. | Already built, EU (Copenhagen/Finland), matches the brief. |
| Billing | Keep Paddle. | Paddle is merchant of record: it collects and pays EU VAT for us. Already integrated. |
| Email | Add a provider-neutral transport for the existing email outbox. Pick an EU-based provider in Phase 6. | The outbox exists (`server/src/services/saas/auth-delivery.ts`) but no mail is sent. An EU provider keeps the "EU hosted" claim honest (LOCK-1029). |
| Public signup | Open only after email verification, abuse limits and Phase 3 audits pass. | Signup without verified email is the main abuse path. |
| Repository visibility | Stay public during development, and make sure that it contains no secrets or customer data. Review before launch. | Public repositories get free GitHub Actions minutes. One full CI run is about 100 jobs. |
| Service owner | The repository owner is the named service owner. Claude prepares the runbooks. | The beta gate requires a named owner. |
| Product claims | Claim only what has verified evidence. Claude drafts the claims, privacy policy and subprocessor list from the code. A lawyer reviews them before launch. | LOCK-1028 and LOCK-1030. Claude is not a lawyer. |
| Usability evidence | Claude writes the test scripts. The owner runs about 5 short sessions with real people before beta. | The beta gate requires human first-value evidence. |
| Skills | Copied `web-design-guidelines` (rules pinned in `guidelines.md`, not fetched at run time) and `playwright-best-practices` into `.claude/skills/`, with MIT licences and source commits. Phase 3 uses the built-in `security-review` instead of the third-party Trail of Bits plugin. | Skills installed on a local machine do not reach cloud sessions. Instructions fetched at run time could change without review. |

## Things the owner will be asked to provide (only when the phase needs them)

| When | What |
|---|---|
| Phase 2-3 | An Anthropic API key, added as a GitHub Actions secret, for the live provider tests. |
| Phase 6 | Accounts: UpCloud, HCP Terraform, Paddle (sandbox first), an email provider, Sentry. The domain name. Company name and address for legal pages. |
| Phase 6 | Slack and Microsoft Teams app registrations, with steps written by Claude. |
| Before beta | A lawyer's review of the privacy policy and terms. About 5 people for short usability sessions. |
